  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Motorized Two-Wheelers (MTWs) dominate personal transport across much of the Global South, yet remain largely overlooked in predictive perception research for autonomous driving and ADAS. We introduce MotoVerse, a benchmark for predictive perception from an MTW's ego-centric perspective. MotoVerse comprises 949 maneuver clips collected in dense, unstructured traffic and trajectory annotations for over 31,000 surrounding road agents, including over 16,000 MTWs, enabling two complementary tasks: Ego-Rider Maneuver Anticipation (ERMA) and Traffic Agent Trajectory Prediction (TATP). Each clip is further annotated with an MTW-specific challenge taxonomy comprising dynamic ego-motion, weak traffic structure and contextual occlusion, enabling challenge-aware evaluation. We benchmark established conventional prediction models, general-purpose Vision-Language Models (VLMs), driving-specific VLMs and vision-language-action models. Our experiments reveal three key insights: (i) video backbones reach most of their full-observation accuracy from the first fifth of a maneuver, whereas zero-shot VLMs need most of the maneuver to be observed; (ii) zero-shot VLMs, whether general-purpose or driving-specific, largely remain close to simple baselines on both tasks; a preliminary LoRA study shows that adaptation lifts anticipation sharply but leaves trajectory prediction behind conventional models; and (iii) the rider's own motion drives forecasting difficulty: for every model, including a static baseline, errors are highest during left turns and rise under dynamic ego-motion. We believe MotoVerse will serve as a challenging benchmark for safer and more robust predictive AI for intelligent two-wheelers. The dataset and evaluation code are publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Intra-Architecture Source Attribution for Stable Diffusion: A New Task with Benchmark Dataset and Baseline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06000)**  `arXiv:2611.06000`  
  _Ashutosh Dayal, Mallika Garg, Debashis Sen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Designing systems that attribute a generated image to its source model has lately become a major area of AI research. Most existing datasets and systems related to image source attribution are targeted towards the detection of architectural signatures of the source models. Distinct from this, focusing on stable diffusion, we introduce a novel task, IntrAA-SD, Intra-Architecture source Attribution for Stable Diffusion, where the source models to be differentiated share the same architectural backbone and differ only due to fine-tuning. The proposed task aligns with the emergence of fine-tuned generators having the same architecture, like Juggernaut XL and RealVis XL, which are fine-tuned versions of Stable Diffusion XL (SDXL). We provide a first-of-its-kind $140$K-image benchmark dataset containing images generated from different fine-tunes of SDXL, along with in-domain and out-of-distribution prompts. We also provide a baseline framework for IntrAA-SD, which leverages a style-conditioned Mixture-of-Experts network. It uses a frozen CLIP backbone to capture the image style and gate the MoE, which processes fine-tuned trace features from a high-resolution LoRA-adapted DINOv2 network. The fine-tuned models used, the prompting strategies considered, and the formation details employed in the novel dataset are elaborated. Our baseline intra-architecture source attribution method is evaluated in terms of attribution accuracy on our dataset, and ablation studies are also performed. Code and dataset is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[FedIA: Instance-Aware Cross-Modal Representations for Federated Prompt Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05994)**  `arXiv:2611.05994`  
  _Xuanjing Zhang, Qi Guo, Yanan Qiao, Yong Han, Junge Bo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Federated Prompt Learning (FPL) enables parameter-efficient adaptation of pre-trained Vision-Language Models across decentralized clients without exchanging raw data. To cope with cross-client heterogeneity, existing FPL methods typically pair a globally shared prompt with a client-specific local prompt. However, this design personalizes only at the client level: the same local prompt is applied to all samples of a client, and the global and local prompts are combined in an input-agnostic, fixed manner. Consequently, it overlooks the substantial visual variations among samples within the same client and cannot adjust the balance between shared and personalized knowledge to each input. To address these limitations, we propose Federated Prompt Learning with Instance-Aware Cross-Modal Representations (FedIA), an FPL framework that learns instance-aware cross-modal representations. Specifically, FedIA first uses global text prompts as client-invariant semantic anchors to select image patches most relevant to each candidate class, suppressing distracting regions. The selected visual evidence then conditions the generation of instance-aware local text representations. Finally, an adaptive global-local fusion module calibrates the contributions of local and global text representations for each image-class pair. In this way, FedIA shifts personalization in FPL from the client level to the instance level while preserving the semantic knowledge shared across clients. Extensive experiments under domain shift, pathological label skew, and Dirichlet-based label skew show that FedIA outperforms FPL baselines, with maximum accuracy gains of 1.44\%-4.37\% over the latest baseline across the three settings. Ablation studies and analyses further verify the complementary contributions of instance-aware representation generation and adaptive global-local fusion.
  </details>

- **[PathoS2VD: From 2D Histopathology to Pseudo Z-Stack Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05974)**  `arXiv:2611.05974`  
  _Jiamu Wang, Keunho Byeon, Ji-Uk Kim, Jin Tae Kwak, Heounjeong Go_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  For decades, diagnostic pathology has relied on thin two-dimensional (2D) tissue sections, despite biological tissues exhibiting inherently three-dimensional (3D) morphological organization. Because a single section captures only one focal plane, important morphological cues along the z-axis are inevitably discarded, limiting tissue representation for both human interpretation and computational analysis. In this work, we propose a generative framework that learns z-axis morphological priors from pathology z-stack data, offering a cost-effective alternative to dedicated volumetric imaging, and transfers this knowledge to conventional single-plane histopathology. Specifically, an image-to-video (I2V) latent diffusion model (LDM) is trained to synthesize a pseudo z-stack from a single 2D image, capturing plausible morphological variations across adjacent focal planes rather than reconstructing physical tissue volumes. To enable deployment on large-scale clinical datasets without native z-stack acquisition, we further introduce a prior-preserving mixture fine-tuning strategy that adapts the model to new domains while retaining the learned z-axis morphological prior. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the synthesized pseudo z-stacks exhibit realistic cross-plane morphological continuity and consistently improve downstream prostate cancer classification compared with conventional 2D representations. Our results suggest that learning transferable z-axis priors from limited z-stack data provides a practical way to enrich routine pathology images with complementary morphological information. Code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[FLINT: Coupling Proximal Initialization and Bounded Stochasticity for Flow-Matching Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05945)**  `arXiv:2611.05945`  
  _Junseo Bang, Daewon Choi, Dong Ju Mun, Se Young Chun_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Flow matching models have emerged as a dominant paradigm for generative posterior sampling in imaging inverse problems. Existing solvers follow two distinct lines of work: trajectory-space methods, which refine sampling dynamics from random initial noise, and source-space methods, which optimize the initial noise itself. While most prior work treats these as independent design choices, we show that they are inherently coupled. We derive a unified stability bound that links initialization proximity and cumulative sampling stochasticity through a multiplicative envelope, complemented by two empirical observations. First, we identify a sufficiency regime in which a moderately optimized proximal source initialization is enough to attain the performance plateau, rendering exhaustive source-space optimization unnecessary. Second, this initial advantage is fragile: it is eroded by excessive stochasticity accumulation during guided sampling, necessitating a mechanism that bounds the cumulative noise injection. These findings motivate FLINT, a two-stage framework that operationalizes this coupling. FLINT establishes a high-fidelity starting point via a manifold-constrained proximal objective in only a few iterations, then performs guided sampling under a bounded stochasticity schedule that helps prevent erasure of the initialization advantage. Across diverse inverse problems, FLINT delivers state of the art perceptual quality and competitive distortion metrics while maintaining superior computational efficiency.
  </details>

- **[3D Banff Glomerulitis Scoring for Kidney Transplant Pathology: Feasibility Study for Volumetric Quantification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05925)**  `arXiv:2611.05925`  
  _Yanfan Zhu, Junchao Zhu, Ruining Deng, Shilin Zhao, Yu Wang, Yaohong Wang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Quantifying glomerular inflammation in kidney-transplant biopsies is traditionally performed on single whole-slide sections using the Banff "most-severe section" rule, despite the inherently three-dimensional nature of lesion distribution. This 2D based assessment might lead to instability when inflammation varies across slices. This paper reports a feasibility study examining the technical feasibility and clinical relevance of extending Banff scoring into three dimensions. To this end, we propose a 3D Banff lesion-scoring framework that aligns serial sections, reconstructs glomerular identities across depth, and integrates inflammatory-cell counts in 3D under three scoring strategies: Compatible, Volumetric, and Deduplicated Volumetric. In the experiments, glomerulitis (g-scores) was used as an example Banff metric and applied to multi-section renal allograft biopsies. Our findings indicate that 3D Banff g-scores are more consistent across sections and, under the semi-automatic setting, the Volumetric strategy correlates more strongly with clinical biomarkers than traditional 2D scores. These results show that 3D volumetric quantification offers promising added value, underscoring the potential benefit of 3D-aware Banff scoring for kidney transplant pathology.
  </details>

- **[DecoupAttn: Decoupled Sparse Attention for Training-Free Acceleration of Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05917)**  `arXiv:2611.05917`  
  _Yanjin Li, Bingbing Qiu, Qiang Huang, Rui Qian, Yanfeng Li, Jian Wang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Sparse attention accelerates few-step video diffusion transformers by computing a few key blocks exactly and compensating for the rest. However, existing sparse attention still faces key challenges, including identification overhead from finding critical blocks at every denoising step, suboptimal budget allocation that sets sparsity budgets uniformly or by local metrics, and cross-step error accumulation as reused masks or clusters go stale. Observing that the errors left on skipped blocks cancel each other once every key is compensated, we propose DecoupAttn, a new paradigm of decoupled sparse attention: semantic clusters compensate every key, and exact attention corrects their error on a few blocks fixed once offline by position. To support this paradigm, we introduce Distortion Budget, a rate-distortion objective for budget allocation that places exact computation across timesteps, layers, and heads where it removes the most distortion that compensation leaves in the final latent. Built on this budget, we further propose Reassign Cache, in which each step reassigns the drifting keys to cached centroids and refreshes compensation while the budgeted blocks stay fixed. Moreover, DecoupAttn is training-free: a few calibration clips compile one plan offline, and inference runs standard block-sparse kernels with one fused compensation kernel. Our code will be released after submission.
  </details>

- **[Do Saliency Maps Fail Dense Breasts? A Chance-Normalized, Confounder-Aware Audit of Explanation Fairness in Mammography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05909)**  `arXiv:2611.05909`  
  _Ahmed Jaheen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Saliency maps, offered as evidence that mammography classifiers use the cancer, are untested in dense breasts, which can hide cancers. We tested whether they locate cancers worse there, and why. On 1,345 malignant CBIS-DDSM views, we scored three CNNs' Grad-CAM, Integrated Gradients and occlusion maps by whether their peak hits the lesion and a chance-normalized lesion-to-background saliency ratio (bits; +1 doubles it). Unadjusted gaps were large: Grad-CAM peaks hit 44% of dense vs. 58% of non-dense lesions. Case mix, chiefly more calcifications in dense breasts, explained about half or more of each gap. Occlusion kept a significant average of -0.56 bit gap. Controls also implicate the classifiers: unadjusted gaps exceeded an image-only conspicuity map's, and models relied less on dense-breast lesions. We recommend reporting density gaps before and after case-mix adjustment, per classifier, with conspicuity and lesion-reliance controls.
  </details>

- **[Feature Puzzles: Single-Image Supervision for Multi-View 3D-Aware Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05874)**  `arXiv:2611.05874`  
  _Jiahao Ma, Qiang Zhang, Miaomiao Liu, David Ahmedt-Aristizabal, Chuong Nguyen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Humans can easily recognize the same objects across different viewpoints of a 3D scene, whereas teaching models such multi-view-consistent perception typically requires videos or calibrated multi-view captures, which are considerably more expensive and less diverse than large-scale monocular data.~In this paper, we introduce Feature Puzzles, a novel framework for learning multi-view-consistent segmentation using only single-image semantic segmentation supervision. Specifically, we propose a simple yet effective pipeline that constructs multi-view-consistent segmentation pseudo-labels through image cropping and geometric transformations. We further introduce a lightweight, plug-and-play network that leverages the multi-view-consistent image features learned by existing pretrained 3D foundation models and the generated pseudo-labels to perform multi-view-consistent segmentation. Importantly, our flexible framework supports various types of prompts provided from one or multiple views and can be readily extended to open-vocabulary 3D segmentation. Under this single-image supervision, our feed-forward model achieves the best mIoU on NVOS, remains competitive on SPIn-NeRF and ScanNet++ against baselines using per-scene optimization or in-domain multi-view training, and demonstrates zero-shot transfer to open-vocabulary 3D segmentation. Visual comparisons are available on our project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Generative AI for urban planning: synthesizing land-use maps with satellite imagery via DualControlNet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05859)**  `arXiv:2611.05859`  
  _Qingqi Song, Yuzhou Chen, Lingqian Hu, Shenhao Wang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Various land-use models characterize land-use patterns and facilitate scenario planning. However, existing models are largely predictive, forecasting transitions based on numeric data without synthesizing new land-use maps from simple text prompts. Meanwhile, generative AI in urban systems remains unimodal, weakly controllable, and evaluated mostly by generic image quality rather than domain-specific metrics. To address these gaps, we introduce DualControlNet, a generative land-use model that jointly synthesizes high-resolution land-use maps and satellite imagery, addressing multimodality, controllability, and verification challenges. Trained on over 40,000 paired tiles from Orlando and Chicago, DualControlNet is evaluated using land-use, spatial consistency, and image quality metrics. Relative to unimodal baselines, DualControlNet improves land-use diversity consistency by 62.8% and building coverage ratio consistency by 16.3%, more than doubles cross-modal spatial consistency, and triples visual quality. Scenario-based experiments demonstrate that DualControlNet supports sustainable development through multidimensional controls, increasing density and diversity, and responding to text prompts like "high-density development." Overall, this work advances land-use modeling via a multimodal, controllable, and verifiable generative framework, lowering technical barriers and enabling intuitive exploration of urban futures.
  </details>

- **[DualCast: Dual-Image Conditioned Diffusion Transformers for Digital Human E-Commerce Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05825)**  `arXiv:2611.05825`  
  _Lifan Jiang, Yibo Zhao, Senlin Cheng, Bolei Xu, Xiaobo Li, Can Ye, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Digital human e-commerce video generation requires preserving product appearance, human identity, and overall video style during dynamic demonstrations. Although recent models support multiple reference images, their general conditioning schemes do not explicitly distinguish the asymmetric roles of "what is presented" and "who presents it." Consequently, generated videos may favor a single reference, resulting in product distortion, identity drift, or single-reference collapse. To address this issue, we propose DualCast, a role-addressed dual-image conditioning framework for video generation. By explicitly modeling the distinct roles of product and human conditions, DualCast reduces role ambiguity and feature competition between references. First, a dual-spatiotemporal slot injection mechanism VAE-encodes the product and human images separately and places their latent features at the front and rear boundaries of the DiT sequence, enabling sequence-level condition separation and directed injection. Second, a mask-guided region-weighted loss strengthens supervision over product, face, and product-face overlap regions, improving generation fidelity in critical areas. Third, image condition dropout enables a single model to support dual-image, product-only, and human-only generation. In addition, we construct a curated dataset of 10K e-commerce videos for model training. Experimental results demonstrate the strong performance of DualCast.
  </details>

- **[Navigational Safety of Marine Surface Vehicles in Restricted Visibility: From Intelligent Vision to Vision-Centered Multi-Sensor Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05818)**  `arXiv:2611.05818`  
  _Ryan Wen Liu, Fengchuan Song, Tianxiang Wang, Guobei Peng, Youngrong Kim, Xian Zhong, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Marine surface vehicles (MSVs) have attracted considerable attention for their commercial, scientific, and military applications. Owing to their favorable performance-to-cost ratio, shipboard cameras are widely used to detect and recognize potentially hazardous situations across diverse operational scenarios. However, visual perception is inevitably degraded by restricted conditions, including irregular motion of the imaging platform, adverse weather, and long imaging distances. To improve situational awareness and navigation capabilities, complementary sensors, such as the automatic identification system (AIS), radar, and light detection and ranging (LiDAR), etc., have become essential shipboard equipment. Multi-sensor fusion can provide richer perceptual information and help ensure navigational safety of MSVs in restricted visibility. This article reviews recent progress from intelligent vision to vision-centered multi-sensor fusion for newcomers and researchers in intelligent ship navigation and intelligent waterborne transportation system (IWTS). After introducing the generation of quality-degraded images in restricted visibility, we will review intelligent vision methods for four key issues, i.e., shipboard video stabilization, visual perception enhancement, object detection and tracking, and navigable area segmentation. We then examine vision-centered multi-sensor fusion methods, including infrared-visible image fusion, radar-vision fusion, LiDAR-vision fusion, AIS-vision fusion, and other multi-sensor fusion scenarios. Open-source datasets and safety-critical applications are also summarized and discussed. Finally, we highlight open challenges and future research directions. The collected maritime datasets are available atthis https URLand will be regularly updated.
  </details>

- **[ReTailor: A Residual Feature Tailoring Framework for Real-Time Cross-Sensor Pansharpening Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05811)**  `arXiv:2611.05811`  
  _Jing-Ze Liang, Wei-Jie Xiao, Zi-Han Cao, Yang-Dong Li, Tian-Yi Hu, Liang-Jian Deng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent pansharpening models achieve strong fusion performance on trained sensors, yet often generalize poorly to unseen sensors. Existing cross-sensor adaptation is limited by target-sensor retraining, which requires target-sensor training pairs and a dedicated training stage, and by per-image zero-shot adaptation, which is computationally expensive due to whole-network optimization for each test image. To address these problems, we propose ReTailor, a real-time sequential cross-sensor adaptation framework. Only a lightweight residual Feature Tailor is rapidly optimized under self-supervised, physics-aware constraints to correct cross-sensor shifts without modifying the well-learned fusion prior. For efficient and effective deployment, ReTailor combines content-aware patch selection, which identifies a few informative patches per image for optimization at negligible cost to improve both quality and efficiency, with rolling residual bootstrap, which inherits sensor-related knowledge across the image stream to further improve quality. Extensive experiments on cross-sensor adaptation settings show that ReTailor mitigates cross-sensor degradation both effectively and efficiently. Moreover, we compare ReTailor with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), a widely used method for fine-tuning, to demonstrate the advantages of our approach. We further evaluate the core principle of ReTailor on two additional remote sensing tasks: super-resolution pansharpening and image segmentation, where it consistently improves performance. In general, ReTailor provides a highly effective and efficient strategy for deploying pansharpening models under real-world cross-sensor degradation, while also offering useful insights into managing cross-sensor degradation in other remote sensing tasks. Code will be released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[ME^2FDiff: Multiscale Expert Enhancing Feature Diffusion in Residual Network for Brain Tumor Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05766)**  `arXiv:2611.05766`  
  _Afrida Rahman, Bijoy Kundu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Automated segmentation of post-treatment glioma from MRI is essential for accurate diagnosis and treatment planning, yet remains difficult. Surgery and chemoradiotherapy cause structural changes in brain like resection cavities, edema, and fibrosis, that alter imaging appearance and mimic tumor recurrence. Contemporary segmentation architectures attain high accuracy with two limitations. First, the bottleneck originates from the deepest encoder stage, so one representation must serve sub-regions that differ widely in size and appearance. Second, they produce the segmentation in a single forward pass with no mechanism for refinement. We propose ME^2FDiff, a single-model architecture, addressing both. An attention-conditioned spectral diffusion block refines the bottleneck. It uses the image-conditioned latent diffusion process with two processes: a spatial pre-attention gate and a spectral fusion block whose timestep-adaptive bandpass filter selects the informative frequency band at each noise level. A multiscale expert-enhanced feature block fuses the intermediate encoder scales into the bottleneck through expert-selected shared tokens, guaranteeing balanced utilization without an auxiliary loss. On the BraTS 2024 post-treatment glioma dataset, ME^2FDiff achieves the best results on every tumor sub-region among all single-model baselines (91.23% Dice, 84.52% IOU, 2.13 mm HD95 on whole-tumor) and surpasses a three-expert ensemble on both overlap metrics at roughly one-third of its inference cost. Against recent state-of-the-art methods retrained under identical conditions, it maintains the strongest performance with the fewest parameters of any competing model. These findings demonstrate that a single, compact network can absorb the specialization, usually sought through ensembling or larger architectures.
  </details>

- **[Learning Hierarchical Patch Splitting Policies for Faster Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05742)**  `arXiv:2611.05742`  
  _Aditya Gupta, Jean Sebastien Dandurand, Kai Qiu, Rohan Choudhury, Laszlo A. Jeni_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision Transformers (ViTs) are bottlenecked by the length of their input token sequence. Standard patchification fixes this length with a single patch size applied across the image, regardless of regional content. Prior work on adaptive patch sizing reduces token count by assigning coarser patches to visually simple regions, but relies on noisy pixel-level heuristics such as local entropy to make patchification decisions. We argue that this compute allocation should be grounded in the model's interpretation of task relevant information, not in raw pixel statistics. We introduce Split Policy Learned Image Tokenization which learns to allocate token resolution based on what the model finds meaningful, producing shorter token sequences that are better matched to task-relevant content. SPLIT matches the accuracy of full-resolution ViT classification baselines while using 30% fewer tokens. The same gains hold on dense prediction: SPLIT achieves competitive accuracy on COCO and ADE20K, demonstrating that semantically aligned patchification generalises well beyond classification.
  </details>

- **[C-WFDM: A ControlNet-Guided Wavelet-Fusion Diffusion Model for Multimodal Brain MRI Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05737)**  `arXiv:2611.05737`  
  _Muhammad Nabi Yasinzai, Remika Mito, Mangor Pedersen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal brain MRI provides complementary anatomical, tissue, and pathological information to support downstream artificial intelligence (AI) applications. However, modality availability is uneven across public and pooled neuroimaging datasets: T1-weighted (T1w) MRI is widely represented, T2-weighted (T2w) and fluid-attenuated inversion recovery (FLAIR) MRI are less consistently available, and gadolinium-enhanced T1-weighted (T1Gd) MRI is relatively scarce. Pooled datasets therefore contain participants with different combinations of acquired MRI contrasts, reflecting variation in the study designs, acquisition protocols, and clinical or research objectives of the contributing studies. Many missing-modality benchmarks simulate incomplete MRI by withholding contrasts from complete four-modality cases, which may not represent the observed availability patterns in pooled datasets. To address this gap, we introduce C-WFDM, a ControlNet extension of our Wavelet-Fusion Diffusion Model (WFDM), for subject-specific missing-modality completion. C-WFDM is built on an earlier BrainScape snapshot comprising 157 public MRI datasets and 45,880 preprocessed scans. The pretrained WFDM backbone retains target-modality and metadata conditioning, while target-specific ControlNet adapters use MRI contrasts from the same participant to guide synthesis. C-WFDM synthesizes T2w from T1w, FLAIR from T1w and T2w, and T1Gd from T1w, T2w, and FLAIR. Acquired conditioning contrasts are used when available, and generated intermediate contrasts are used only when required. We compare C-WFDM with ALDM, cWDM, and Pix2Pix using paired structural similarity index (SSIM), peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR), and mean absolute error (MAE). In the BrainScape completion experiment, C-WFDM achieves the best T2w and FLAIR results among the evaluated models, with SSIM values of 0.9232 and 0.8904, respectively.
  </details>

- **[Mitigating Spurious Invariance in Contrastive Learning: A Probabilistic Self-Supervised Learning Framework for Medical Image Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05734)**  `arXiv:2611.05734`  
  _Wei Wei, Yading Yuan_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Owing to the prohibitive cost of manual annotation for enormous medical images, self-supervised learning (SSL) has gained substantial attention and shown promise in various medical imaging tasks. Among SSL approaches, contrastive learning has emerged as a prominent one, encouraging models to encode semantic information that remains invariant between different augmented views. However, this invariance can be spurious for medical images, as subtle but critical medical features can be suppressed during augmentations. In this paper, we mitigate this issue by encoding the learned feature embeddings as probability distributions rather than fixed vectors, thus capturing a richer and more nuanced spectrum of information. Furthermore, we propose a unified probabilistic framework that seamlessly integrates multiple SSL approaches. Our method achieves superior performance over the state-of-the-art SSL methods on various downstream tasks across multiple datasets, including medical image segmentation and classification. We further validate the method through additional analyses of robustness to stronger augmentations, label efficiency under limited fine-tuning labels, and computational trade-offs. The code is released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[GeoDIVE: Geospatial Descent Informed by Value Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05697)**  `arXiv:2611.05697`  
  _Hamza Iqbal, Dan Cher, Eric Xing, Pranavi Kolouju, Abby Stylianou, Robert Pless, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Finding specific objects, structures, and spatial patterns supports critical work in agriculture, infrastructure planning, environmental monitoring, and disaster response. These features, however, are often dispersed across very large regions and span dramatically different spatial scales, which defines a search space that is costly to embed, maintain, and search naively. We introduce GeoDIVE, a model that treats a multiresolution satellite image gallery as a forest and uses best-first traversal to determine which tiles to encode. At each visited node, a lightweight model then predicts an optimistic estimate of the best query similarity hidden among its descendants using the query and subtree representations. In evaluations with image and open-vocabulary text queries, GeoDIVE matches exhaustive search at 10% of the encoding cost for image queries and leads all tested baselines at every budget in both modalities. To support future evaluation of multiscale geospatial search, we also release object-level image and text queries for Git-10M, constructed from Microsoft building footprints and OpenStreetMap polygons with VLM-generated captions. Source code and models will be made publicly available.
  </details>

- **[Anchored Slice Propagation with Boundary Transport for 3D CT Segmentation from Three Orthogonal Annotated Slices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05693)**  `arXiv:2611.05693`  
  _Tushar Kataria, Md Rakibul Haque, Shireen Y. Elhabian_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Sli2Vol propagates a binary mask through affinities between adjacent slices, but an error can persist because each prediction becomes the input to the next step. Three orthogonal annotations reduce dependence on any one chain, yet they offer more than three starting masks: where a chain crosses another annotated plane, the correct label is already known. Those labels could correct drift during propagation and indicate how much to trust each chain at fusion. Between annotated planes, the information carried by the chain may matter as well. A binary mask records foreground membership but discards distance to the boundary, a cue that could help follow gradual shape changes betweenthis http URLaddress these two sources of error with a fixed pretrained affinity backbone. We anchor each chain by correcting its prediction where it crosses another annotated plane, then use its agreement with those labels to weight fusion. We compare this anchored mask with two ways of transporting boundary geometry: a truncated signed-distance field (SDF) propagated by dense affinity averaging, and a contour whose vertices follow motion cues from the same affinity. The contour tracker starts from a hand-crafted rule and learns a motion correction from supervoxel pseudo-objects in 506 CT volumes, without manual organ labels. On 2,091 held-out structure instances from approximately 403 CT scans spanning 17 abdominal structures, anchoring raises mean Dice from 0.737 with orthogonal mask propagation to 0.769 and improves every structure. Beyond the anchored mask, learned contour tracking improves Dice by 5.7\% for the esophagus and 5.9\% for the postcava, while SDF transport improves right-adrenal Dice by 2.8\%. The anchored methods have similar mean Dice (0.769--0.772); contour tracking performs worse on the kidneys, stomach, and hepatic vessel tree.
  </details>

- **[TRUST-Stereo: Teacher-Assisted Robust Uncertainty-Guided Search and Transfer for Fast Stereo Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05689)**  `arXiv:2611.05689`  
  _Pouya Sohrabipour, Yihong Feng, Chaitanya Kumar Reddy Pallerla, Tuan Dang, Ngan Le, Dongyi Wang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Coarse-to-fine stereo matching enables efficient disparity estimation but remains sensitive to errors in propagated disparity, which may place ground-truth correspondences outside subsequent local search ranges. We propose TRUST-Stereo (Teacher-Assisted Robust Uncertainty-Guided Search and Transfer for Fast Stereo Matching), which addresses this limitation through an Uncertainty-Guided Residual Search Module (UGRSM). UGRSM estimates the reliability of propagated disparity and predicts residual offsets to adaptively relocate local cost-volume centers. Additionally, we introduce a two-stage Senior-Junior Teacher Assistant knowledge distillation strategy to transfer rich monocular representations from a large foundation model to a lightweight backbone to achieve fast stereo matching performance. Experiments on KITTI 2012, KITTI 2015, ETH3D, and Middlebury demonstrate strong zero-shot generalization. Using only 1.4M synthetic stereo pairs, TRUST-Stereo achieves state-of-the-art results on several benchmark metrics, outperforming competing efficient methods trained with substantially larger datasets, including real-world pseudo-labeled samples. Specifically, it achieves a KITTI 2015 EPE of 0.93, ETH3D Bad 1.0 of 2.13% and Middlebury Bad 2.0 of 6.79% while maintaining inference at approximately 50 ms per stereo pair.
  </details>

- **[Toward an Optimal Configuration for Tree Species Classification from Bark Images: A Leakage-Safe Ablation Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05680)**  `arXiv:2611.05680`  
  _Maeve Côté_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Background: The automatic identification of tree species from bark photographs is of practical interest for forestry inventory and the wood-processing industry, yet the design choices that govern the accuracy of deep-learning approaches have not been studied systematically, and some prior results may be inflated by leakage of images of the same trunk across training and test partitions. Methods: A systematic ablation study is conducted on the public BarkNet 1.0 dataset (20 species) under the established tree-level, leakage-safe partitioning scheme. Using ConvNeXt-V2 backbones, the following factors are varied in turn and evaluated by five-fold cross-validation: patch size, the strategy by which per-patch predictions are aggregated into an image-level decision, the training regime (single- versus two-stage), backbone capacity, the whole-image baseline, and the number of patches available at inference. Results: Per-patch accuracy rises monotonically with patch size (from 75.6% at 160 px to 89.6% at 512 px), and image-level accuracy rises in step, plateauing near 94.9% around 512 px. Learned aggregation exceeds majority voting by at most 0.7 pp, an advantage that shrinks as patches grow and attention becomes near-uniform. Backbone capacity (pico to tiny) alters accuracy by less than a point, and two-stage training offers no advantage over one-stage. Whole-image classification (91.9%) trails patch-based aggregation, since it admits no bag to average over; accuracy increases with the number of patches at inference, saturating around 64. Conclusions: The study yields a simple recommended configuration: patches as large as possible while leaving enough to average over (around 512 px here), with a nano backbone. It also quantifies the second-order role of learned aggregation and model capacity, providing a reproducible reference point for practitioners and future research
  </details>

- **[NitroFusion2: Bringing Adversarial Loss to the Text Stream of Multimodal Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05648)**  `arXiv:2611.05648`  
  _Dar-Yen Chen, Yi-Zhe Song_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Adversarial diffusion distillation has conventionally relied on image features for discrimination. We revisit this choice for multimodal diffusion transformers (MMDiTs), whose text stream becomes image-dependent through joint attention. We introduce Text-Stream Adversarial Distillation (TSAD), which exploits intermediate text-token states as a new adversarial representation. Because the text stream has already read the image while remaining tied to the text condition, changing that condition changes what information it extracts from the image. Building on this property, we introduce Aspect-Cued Discrimination (ACD), which conditions the discriminator on different aspects of the sample, such as typography, objects, or style, to steer which properties are emphasized during adversarial training. Building on the expert-discriminator design of NitroFusion, we integrate TSAD and ACD in NitroFusion2. NitroFusion2 brings few-step distillation from the conventional 4-8-step regime down to just 1-2 steps at comparable quality. At one step, it produces sharp rendered text and rich fine-grained details, making one-step generation practical rather than merely a stress test for distillation. At two steps, it remains competitive across the evaluated benchmarks. These results establish the MMDiT text stream as a powerful and steerable representation for adversarial diffusion distillation.
  </details>

- **[ARIDA - Active railway infrastructure dataset annotation using neural network predictions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05646)**  `arXiv:2611.05646`  
  _Maximilian Dornik, Christian Sieberichs, Simon Geerkens, Alexander Braun_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Modern railway perception requires large comprehensive image datasets, but creating them is slowed by manual labeling, especially for track polylines and other complex infrastructure geometry. This paper presents the Active Railway Infrastructure Dataset Annotation framework, a railway-specific semi-automated pipeline that converts instance predictions from a mask scoring instance segmentation network into bounding-boxes, polygons, polylines, and paired polylines. To keep automatic track geometry annotation reliable without ground truth, it applies confidence thresholds and an unique contour-agreement quality score as an acceptance gate. An active learning loop ranks remaining images by uncertainty and adapts the number of manual queries per cycle. This is achieved through a novel quantity control mechanism, in which easy images are auto-accepted and human effort targets hard cases. Experiments on disjoint, diverse imagery and time-sequential, redundant imagery quantify effort reduction and accuracy trade-offs, achieving about two thirds reduction in the disjoint regime and up to 93% in the sequential regime. The study also provides guidance for initial label budgets and stopping to avoid over-annotation.
  </details>

- **[Spatio-Temporal Multi-Order Interaction for Video Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05633)**  `arXiv:2611.05633`  
  _Kevin D. Nguetche, Lilian Hollard, Eric Granger, Marco Pedersoli_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Many temporal approaches for video recognition rely on explicit cross-channel aggregation projections, which have quadratic complexity in the number of channels, introducing significant computational overhead. In addition, the spatio-temporal representation used for video recognition is biased either toward local order interactions (for CNNs) or toward global order interactions (for transformers). Starting from the multi-order spatial representation of MogaNet, our method introduces two complementary contributions to tackle these problems. First, for the temporal modelling, we introduce parallel multi-order processing with depthwise-only convolutions and implicit gating-based aggregation, which is linear in the number of channels, inducing a considerable computational reduction. Second, we propose a dual interaction between spatial and temporal dimensions, which allows a better spatio-temporal representation without increasing the computational cost. By combining these two contributions, our proposed VideoMogaNet achieves performance on par with or better than state-of-the-art action recognition approaches across multiple video benchmarks (UCF101, HMDB51, Kinetics-400, Diving48), while considerably reducing the cost of the temporal branch.
  </details>

- **[CAPTOR: text-encoder-free grounding by counting caption co-occurrence over the visual words of a frozen encoder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05599)**  `arXiv:2611.05599`  
  _Elad Yoshai, Natan T. Shaked_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Grounding a text query in an image needs a matrix scoring every term against every patch. Vision-language models compute it with an aligned text encoder, which many image encoders, such as histopathology-related models trained on slides alone, lack. The Caption-Anchored Patch-Term Occurrence Representation (CAPTOR) estimates the matrix without a text encoder. It clusters a frozen image encoder's patch tokens into visual words and describes each captioned image by its share of patches in each word. A term signature is the standardized difference of those shares between images whose captions mention the term and the rest. The matrix factorizes into a term-by-word table and a query-independent image factor, so an image is encoded once and a new term adds one table row. Against every training-free baseline, CAPTOR leads on Common Objects in Context (COCO), Pascal Visual Object Classes (VOC) and Cityscapes. On COCO, scored on the cells one object owns, it reaches 0.565 mean intersection over union (mIoU) against 0.502. On all three, it is also level with a text alignment trained on the same backbone, which needs gradients, a text encoder and several times the captions. On held-out breast-cancer slides, it exceeds the strongest baseline scoring patches with a pathology text encoder by 0.160 mIoU. Read through CAPTOR, UNI, an encoder trained on slides alone, exceeds such baselines by 0.132 on a common grid. Counting can therefore give a text interface to any image encoder trained without language, wherever captioned images exist.
  </details>

- **[Multimodal Fusion Methods for Whole-Slide Images and Clinical Data in Inflammatory Bowel Disease](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05579)**  `arXiv:2611.05579`  
  _Ankana Banerjee, Mark van der Giezen, Ole Gunnar Aasprong, Emiel A. M. Janssen, Øyvind Steinsbø, Tore Grimstad, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal AI fusion of histology and clinical data is an active research area in computational pathology, but fusion methods are rarely compared under identical conditions and across different clinical endpoints. We present a modular framework in which slide-level embeddings, obtained by training an attention-based aggregator over patch embeddings from a frozen foundation model, and per-visit clinical embeddings are fed into 15 interchangeable fusion methods with a shared output dimension and prediction head (except decision-level methods). Using a prospective cohort of newly diagnosed inflammatory bowel disease patients, we predicted STRIDE-II-aligned remission outcomes from baseline or follow-up visits. The best fusion method reached macro-F1 of 0.57-0.75, outperforming both single-modality models in four of six scenarios, and differed by outcome and input. Image reliance, the drop in balanced accuracy when the slide embedding is removed, tended to follow the remission type - highest for histological and lowest for endoscopic remission; methods with similar accuracy relied on the slide to very different degrees.
  </details>

- **[Whose Values Are Generated? Measuring Visual Value Leakage in Multilingual Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05578)**  `arXiv:2611.05578`  
  _Zucan Lyu, Binwei Yao, Rheeya Uppaal, Jing Yao, Xiaoyuan Yi, Junjie Hu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-image (T2I) systems serve users across languages and cultural contexts. However, evaluations of cultural representation focus mainly on recognizable elements such as landmarks, clothing, and activities, overlooking the cultural values that generated images convey. To bridge this gap, we introduce VisionValueBench, a multilingual benchmark that operationalizes six of Hofstede's cultural value dimensions (e.g., individualism versus collectivism) to investigate how T2I systems frame neutral social situations through a particular cultural orientation. We find that value-neutral prompts, which admit diverse cultural interpretations, nonetheless yield images that favor particular value orientations, a phenomenon we call visual value leakage. We evaluate prompts varying in language and geographic cues across 960 social scenarios and six T2I configurations. Generated images consistently favor the same value orientations across all six dimensions, for example, equality over hierarchy (low power distance), individual autonomy over group obligation (individualism), and enjoyment over restraint (indulgence). Prompt language has limited influence on these orientations, whereas geographic cues shift some dimensions toward the associated cultural values while largely preserving the prevailing orientations. These findings reveal substantial homogeneity in the cultural values conveyed by T2I systems worldwide.
  </details>

- **[ChangeJEPA: Predict Before You Compare for Remote Sensing Change Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05570)**  `arXiv:2611.05570`  
  _Buddhi Wijenayake, Dhananjaya Jayasundara, Vishal M. Patel_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Remote sensing change detection is typically formulated as Siamese feature comparison, where two images are encoded independently and a decoder determines which discrepancies correspond to meaningful change. This is challenging because the encoders preserve both true scene changes and nuisance variations such as illumination, seasonal, radiometric, and geometric differences. We introduce ChangeJEPA, a bitemporal joint-embedding predictive architecture that reformulates change detection as latent post-state prediction. Given a pre-change image and partial post-change evidence, ChangeJEPA predicts the full post-change representation under two complementary conditions: a prior hypothesis derived from the pre-change scene and an updated hypothesis conditioned on observed post-change context. A joint-embedding predictive loss aligns both hypotheses with the target latent representation, encouraging semantic consistency while suppressing transient appearance variations. Change is then inferred from the disagreement among the pre-change representation, the observed post-change target, and the predicted latent states, capturing complementary evidence of change, prior surprise, contextual update, and prediction reliability. Across SYSU-CD, LEVIR-CD+, and CDD, ChangeJEPA consistently outperforms its decoder matched Siamese baseline by up to 1.51 IoU points and achieves the best IoU and F1-score on all three benchmarks, demonstrating the advantage of latent post-state prediction over direct feature comparison. Page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Role-based Effectiveness of an Epistemic Uncertainty Estimate in Training Models for Semantic Segmentation of 3D LiDAR Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05569)**  `arXiv:2611.05569`  
  _Krishnakumar N, Jaya Sreevalsan-Nair_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Point cloud segmentation is a fundamental task in 3D data analysis, with applications across robotics, augmented and virtual reality, and remote sensing. However, the inherent sparsity, noise, and unordered nature of point clouds introduce significant challenges, particularly in achieving reliable classification. While the state-of-the-art rely on training models for explicit modeling of uncertainty, there is a gap in exploring different roles that estimated uncertainty using generative approaches can play. This work investigates the impact of deterministic uncertainty in point cloud segmentation by explicitly modeling epistemic uncertainty and using it in different roles, namely, as a feature and in loss function. We evaluate a baseline artificial neural network (ANN) and two uncertainty-aware variants (uncertainty as an additional input feature and as a per-sample confidence weight in the training loss) on four large-scale LiDAR benchmarks spanning airborne and mobile acquisition, namely ISPRS Vaihingen, AHN3, Paris-Lille-3D, and Toronto-3D, together with PointNet++ and RandLA-Net references and their uncertainty-augmented counterparts under canonical spatially disjoint train and test splits. Under this protocol, uncertainty-aware modeling improves mean Intersection over Union, recovers minority classes that the baseline ANN never predicts at all, and in half of the configurations improves the alignment between prediction error and estimated uncertainty as measured by Patch Accuracy versus Patch Uncertainty. Our findings indicate that uncertainty can play multiple roles in the segmentation pipeline rather than as a device for improving accuracy alone.
  </details>

- **[Discarded or Unreachable? Diagnosing and Repairing Spatial Relation Failure in Vision--Language Similarity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05565)**  `arXiv:2611.05565`  
  _Mahdi Mostajabdaveh, Zirui Zhou_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Contrastive vision-language models score left/right relations near chance, but the symptom has two causes that call for opposite repairs: the relation may survive in the pooled embedding yet be unreachable by native image-text similarity, or pooling may discard it while the dense features retain it. On clean two object photographs, across ten encoders, the lateral polarity is linearly decodable from the flip odd component of the pooled vector at 0.77-0.99, yet a search over a million captions leaves nine of the ten at chance under native scoring: an access failure. On controlled C4 orbits, where the relation is the only thing that varies, pooling keeps the arrangement axis at 0.998-1.000 and loses its polarity, which the patch grid retains: a pooling failure. Both sit downstream of the dense features, so one readout covers both. Our zero parameter, query conditioned spatial moment recomputes the geometry from the patch grid and stores it as a vector beside the native embedding. On a 118,287 image index it raises left/right pairwise accuracy against reversed geometry negatives from 0.51-0.53 to 0.81-0.96 and conditional R@1 among 21 category matched candidates from 0.05 to 0.37-0.75, at under a millisecond per batched query, while reproducing the native ranking exactly for queries that name no relation.
  </details>

- **[Lesion Overlap Ranks PET Segmentation Models but Does Not Certify Patient-Level Tumor Burden](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05564)**  `arXiv:2611.05564`  
  _Tushar Kataria, Gerald Sabin, Ponnuswamy Sadayappan, Shireen Y. Elhabian_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Metabolic tumor volume (MTV) summarizes whole-body PET disease burden for prognostic stratification and treatment assessment. Automated MTV is useful only if its estimate is reliable for the individual patient, yet segmentation models are usually evaluated with overlap metrics such as Dice rather than burden error. We examined whether overlap metrics identify unreliable MTV estimates using 1,014 autoPET FDG PET/CT studies. We evaluated LesionTracer predictions held out from fine-tuning, compared five selected released models on 489 tumor-positive patients, and examined the findings in 597 PSMA studies. Across the five FDG models, ranking by higher mean Dice gave the same order as ranking by lower mean relative MTV error ($\rho=-1.00$, exact $p=0.017$). At the patient level, however, out-of-fold Dice tracked relative MTV error strongly ($\rho=-0.75$), yet 82 of 489 patients (16.8\%) fell on opposite sides of the Dice 0.80 and MTV-error 25\% thresholds. Within the training-exposed released models, correlations ranged from $-0.47$ to $-0.74$ over narrower, high-Dice ranges. Out of fold, opposing false-positive and false-negative volumes cancelled about one third of spatial error in net MTV. In 236 of 489 patients, each of the five models missed at least one lesion, though not necessarily the same lesion. Thus, agreement in average model rankings did not establish the reliability of individual MTV estimates or reveal shared lesion misses. PET segmentation benchmarks should report patient-level MTV error and missed-lesion counts alongside Dice and other overlap metrics.
  </details>

- **[How Finely Can Vision-Language Models See Biology? Bio-VQA: A Benchmark for Taxonomic, Morphological, Spatial and Individual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05559)**  `arXiv:2611.05559`  
  _S M Rayeed, Qian Ma, Charles V. Stewart, Yao Ma_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In biodiversity monitoring, a single image raises several questions: which taxon, how many, which trait, and whether the individual has been seen before. Vision-language models (VLMs) could answer all of these from the image, but their ability is largely untested in biology, as existing benchmarks focus mostly on species identification within a few taxonomic groups. We present Bio-VQA, the largest biological visual question answering (VQA) benchmark and the only one to span four aspects of biological visual understanding: hierarchical taxonomic reasoning, fine-grained attribute grounding, quantitative spatial reasoning, and individual re-identification. Bio-VQA contains 1.86M questions over 217K images from 11 public datasets, and extends right-or-wrong scoring to graded scoring, so a near-miss counts for more than a distant one. Evaluating 18 open-weight models from 3.75B to 38.4B parameters, we find that general visual capabilities do not transfer to biological organisms: the best model counts animals exactly on 41.7% of images with 2 to 10 individuals, less than half its accuracy on everyday objects at the same counts. Taxonomic accuracy falls to one sixth, from 0.72 at phylum to 0.12 at scientific name, and scaling helps mostly at coarse ranks. The gap to a domain specialist widens accordingly, from 0.731 against BioCLIP-2's 0.874 at class (1.2x) to 0.269 against 0.544 at scientific name (2x). On trait size measurement, 15 of the 18 models do no better than an image-blind baseline that predicts one fixed number for every specimen, and on re-identification, 17 of the 18 models fall below chance under adversarial conditions. These failures persist across a tenfold range of model size. We hope Bio-VQA becomes a standard test of how well VLM capabilities transfer to the biological domain.
  </details>

- **[From Activation to Intervention: Interpreting Sparse Visual Features through Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05522)**  `arXiv:2611.05522`  
  _Jianyang Gu, Jacob Beattie, Mridul Khurana, Julia Chae, Xi Xiao, Cheng Zhang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) decompose visual representations into individual features, offering a promising basis for explaining model behavior. Interpreting these features typically relies on images or pixel regions that most strongly activate them. However, this evidence becomes ambiguous when multiple features with different semantics frequently activate in the same locations, such as texture and color in the same patterns. We propose Reconstructive SAE Intervention (RecSAEI), a framework that shifts feature explanation from passive observation to active visual reconstruction. By intervening on representations along SAE decoder directions and reconstructing the visual output, we provide direct evidence of a feature's semantic meaning. We demonstrate that our method successfully disambiguates feature meanings across multiple datasets and vision encoders. Furthermore, our framework grounds [CLS] SAE interventions in a sparse set of patch SAE decoder directions. Beyond stable target response, this attribution also exposes the compositional relationship between elementary visual patterns and high-level semantics. Our results advocate for intervention reconstruction as an essential complementary signal for explaining neural networks.
  </details>

- **[From Tokens to Trajectories: Parameter-Efficient Continuous Adaptation of Unified Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05451)**  `arXiv:2611.05451`  
  _Gayatri Deshmukh, Onkar Susladkar, Adheesh Juvekar, Halil Kilicoglu, Ismini Lourentzou_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While discrete tokenization in Unified Multimodal Models (UMMs) establishes strong semantic reasoning, it introduces a strict quantization bottleneck that degrades fine-grained visual details, often resulting in structurally accurate but artifact-heavy images. We introduce FlowShift, a parameter-efficient post-training framework that bypasses this bottleneck by converting pre-trained discrete UMMs into continuous rectified-flow generators. Instead of discarding the backbone's semantic priors, FlowShift replaces discrete visual interfaces with continuous latent projections and applies LoRA to the attention layers. To efficiently boost visual quality without semantic drift, we propose Dual-Objective Velocity Vector Distillation (DOVD), which aligns the model with a high-fidelity continuous teacher and ground-truth continuous trajectories. This approach drastically improves perceptual quality while preserving prompt adherence and reasoning. When applied to leading discrete UMMs, FlowShift significantly improves COCO-30K FID and aesthetic scores, while retaining over 99.5% of multimodal understanding capabilities. Ablations confirm DOVD drives generation quality. Ultimately, FlowShift achieves high-fidelity continuous generation without sacrificing pre-trained semantic grounding.
  </details>

- **[SO(3)-Equivariant Learning on CAD Boundary Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05449)**  `arXiv:2611.05449`  
  _Matteo Ballegeer, Dries F. Benoit_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Learning on boundary representations (B-reps) requires robustness to the pose of a part, since CAD models are not stored in a canonical orientation. Existing B-rep networks either operate on world-frame coordinates and normals, and thus degrade under rotation, or encode each face in a local reference frame. Local frames yield invariance, but reduce inter-face messages to scalars and discard the relative orientation of adjacent faces. We propose SO3brep, an SO(3)-equivariant graph neural network on the B-rep face-adjacency graph. Node features are computed in local frames and mapped into a shared frame by Wigner D-matrices. Clebsch-Gordan tensor products couple these features with the directions between adjacent faces, so that relative orientation is preserved throughout message passing and the output remains SE(3)-invariant. Across three face-segmentation and two classification benchmarks with randomly rotated test parts, SO3brep achieves the highest accuracy among the compared B-rep networks and equivariant point cloud networks, with the fewest parameters. Its advantage is largest with few labeled parts, a setting typical of industrial CAD. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SurgTransfer: Multi-Step Latent Action Modeling for Action Transfer in Surgical World Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05443)**  `arXiv:2611.05443`  
  _Saurabh Koju, Love Panta, Deepak Thapa, Rudra P. K. Poudel, Prajwal Ghimire, Angus Watson, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Surgical world models have the potential to provide intra-operative guidance, accelerate risk-free training and facilitate transfer of expert surgical maneuvers. However, the use of world models for transferring surgical expertise has been largely unexplored. Moreover, recent approaches in this domain have mainly relied on ground truth kinematics actions or expert annotations for specific tasks, limiting their applicability beyond the narrow range of tasks with ground-truth annotations. In contrast, existing works in the natural domain have shown promising results in learning latent actions solely from unlabeled videos and transferring the learned actions across contexts. We observe that this paradigm is short-sighted for the surgical domain, failing to capture the hierarchical and goal-directed structure of surgical procedures. Thus, we propose a novel multi-step action learning framework to learn context-invariant latent actions that are easily transferable across various surgical contexts. We perform extensive experiments on the publicly available datasets, SurgToolLoc-2022 and PitVis-2023, verifying our approach with both quantitative metrics and qualitative evaluation by surgical experts. We have published our code and the implementation details atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[PointNDF: Cascaded Neural Displacement Fields for Point Cloud Denoising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05411)**  `arXiv:2611.05411`  
  _Michael Y. Wang, Pingping Cai_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Point cloud denoising aims to recover clean geometry from noisy 3D observations while preserving both fine surface details and the underlying spatial distribution of the points. Many learning-based methods adopt a two-stage pipeline that first extracts point-wise features from the noisy input and subsequently predicts point-wise displacements via simple regressions. However, these approaches often lack robust feature guidance and distribution-aware geometric refinement, causing them to fail at maintaining optimal point uniformity and geometric quality. To address these limitations, we propose PointNDF, a denoising framework that combines patch-level geometric context with local displacement prediction. Specifically, a Point Transformer V3 (PTv3) encoder aggregates multiscale geometric information into a global patch descriptor. Rather than directly regressing an unconstrained displacement vector, a Neural Displacement Field (NDF) fuses this global patch descriptor with per-point neighborhood features. It then predicts each displacement through bounded-vector and scale factor branches, thereby achieving a superior, more uniform point distribution. Three cascaded NDF stages propagate local features and progressively refine the point positions. Experiments on PU-Net and ModelNet10 demonstrate competitive denoising accuracy and favorable inference efficiency. The source code is publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Augmentation with Reinforcement Transformation for Low-textured Scientific Sketch Image Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05408)**  `arXiv:2611.05408`  
  _Zhiqiang Wang, Sheri L. Barton, Jennifer H. Johnson, Yi He, Xingquan Zhu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Low-textured sketch images are widely used in scientific applications, including wildlife conservation, archaeological illustration, and forensic composites. Learning from and retrieving such images is challenging because they lack detailed surface textures and visual variations commonly exploited by modern deep learning models. This paper proposes Augmentation with Reinforcement Transformation (ART) to fine-tune pretrained models to learn effective representations for accurate sketch image retrieval. Our key observation is that sketches are particularly sensitive to image transformations, such as rotation, flipping, zooming, and stretching. While some transformations preserve their structural integrity, others may introduce distortions that hinder representation learning. Instead of randomly choosing transformations to construct fine-tuning views, ART formulates augmentation selection as a multi-armed bandit (MAB) problem, where transformations are treated as arms and their effectiveness is continuously evaluated through rewards. This enables ART to adaptively construct reward-guided view banks before self-supervised fine-tuning; the subsequent representation loss does not feed back to ART. On Florida manatee sketches provided by Mote Marine Laboratory, ART achieves 71.40% average Top-1 accuracy for transformed-scar retrieval, compared with 68.83% for random augmentation selection, and 55.84% for location-aware sketch retrieval, compared with 18.18% for frozen CLIP.
  </details>

- **[PEEK: One-Step-Look-Ahead Exposure Bias Correction for Diffusion Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05407)**  `arXiv:2611.05407`  
  _Yunwei Bai, Ying Kiat Tan, Tsuhan Chen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion image generators are successful due to accurate denoising networks and strong numerical samplers. Yet the two components are not fully matched. During sampling, the denoiser predicts for the next step based on flawed input that differ much from the real data. This train-test gap, or exposure bias, caps image generation quality (i.e., blurry, lacking in details or awkward in shape). To mitigate the problem, we propose PEEK, a novel training-free deterministic sampler plugin. At each eligible denoising step, PEEK first performs a provisional one-step update, evaluates the denoiser once at the look-ahead state, and interpolates it with the one-step-later prediction to form the next noisy state. By taking the additional step, we obtain a generally more-image-like prediction from the higher-signal noisy state, mitigating the exposure bias. To optimize the interpolation positions, PEEK searches for an interpolation schedule search made efficient by the prefix and baseline noise-schedule-rebasing caches. On 8 pixel-space and latent-space diffusion models with unconditional, label-guided, and text-guided settings across various output resolutions, PEEK consistently improves over NFE-matched baselines, with FID improvements up to 49.3\% at minimal extra computation cost (e.g., the one-time-per-model calibration takes at most 2.2 minutes across our experiments.). Project website is atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[When Does Sharing Work? Scaling a Shared SigLIP2 Across Image, Video, Audio, and Audio-Visual Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05406)**  `arXiv:2611.05406`  
  _Xiaofei Sun, Peng Wang, Huayao Liu, Xiaoxiao Xu, Jun Song, Deqing Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Contrastive pretraining has advanced image-text understanding, yet image, video, and audio encoders are still trained independently, with cross-modal alignment realized mainly through post-hoc binding to a frozen anchor space. We present Omni-Encoder, a general multimodal encoder that processes image, video, audio, and audio-visual inputs within a single SigLIP-2 Vision Transformer with fully shared parameters. Unlike anchor-centric binding or per-modality adapters, all modalities are optimized jointly end-to-end, and the framework is organized from input to training. A modality-native input stage first maps each signal into the shared embedding space, including a 1D-convolutional audio tokenizer that converts mel-spectrograms into tokens aligned with visual patches. A multimodal rotary position embedding parameterized by absolute timestamps then places visual frames and audio segments in a unified spatiotemporal coordinate system. Finally, alternating-modality training interleaves image, video, audio, and temporally aligned audio-visual batches and accumulates their gradients into one update, which we find central to stable joint optimization. Omni-Encoder achieves competitive results on 16 benchmarks across all four modalities with one shared parameter set, and joint training improves each modality over its separately trained counterpart. Code and models will be publicly released.
  </details>

- **[mmHand: Toward Pixel-Level-Accuracy Hand Localization Using a Single Commodity mmWave Device](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05404)**  `arXiv:2611.05404`  
  _Xiaoyu Zhang, Zhengxiong Li, Chenhan Xu, Luchuan Song, Huining Li, Hongfei Xue, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The hand localization problem has been a longstanding focus due to its many applications. The task involves modeling the hand as a singular point and determining its position within a defined coordinate system. However, due to data modality limitations, existing hand localization technologies face several challenges. For example, vision-based localization raises privacy concerns, while wearable-based methods compromise user comfort. In this paper, we introduce mmHand, a new device-free, privacy-preserving dynamic hand localization system with pixel-level accuracy, using a single commodity mmWave device. We first propose a mmImage generation tool to fully extract spatial information from raw mmWave data and introduce a novel 2D image-format representation of mmWave data. Next, we design a framework that provides a new quality evaluation method and pixel space labeling for the mmWave data. Finally, we present a cross-modality spatial feature-enhanced model with high spatial feature extraction capabilities, which can accurately localize hand positions at the pixel level in the mmWave radar U-V pixel coordinate system. We evaluate the system with experiments on 12 subjects in three scenarios, and the results across four metrics demonstrate the effectiveness of our hand localization system.
  </details>

- **[Visual Forensic Detection of Fabricated Bangla News Photocards using Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05403)**  `arXiv:2611.05403`  
  _Anindya Kundu, Kabya Mithun Saha, Md. Muhaiminul Islam, Fayek Ahmed, Aminul Kader Bulbul, Muhammad Ibrahim_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In Bangladesh, much localized misinformation spreads as photocards: single-image news summaries that imitate the branding of well-known news organizations. Because the misleading message lives in the pixels rather than in machine-readable text, text-based fact-checking tools cannot see it. We recast photocard fake-news detection as visual image forgery detection. We build a dataset of 969 authentic and 1,077 fabricated Bangla photocards scraped from public Facebook pages: authentic cards from the verified pages of seventeen national television broadcasters, fabricated ones from pseudo-broadcaster pages, anonymous profiles, and public groups. Sixteen photometric and morphological transformations per image grow the corpus to 32,736 samples. A fully connected ANN reaches only about 52% accuracy, its loss stuck at ln(2) ~ 0.693, showing that flattening the image destroys the spatial cues forgery detection needs. A compact, fully batch-normalized four-block CNN with 489,954 parameters, about 1,644x smaller than the ANN, is evaluated with leakage-free stratified group five-fold cross-validation, which keeps every augmented version of a card in one fold. Under this protocol the CNN reaches 87.45% +/- 0.53% pooled accuracy from scratch and 88.75% +/- 1.55% when its classifier head is warm-started from the ANN. An ImageNet-pretrained YOLOv8n-cls backbone (1.44M parameters) fine-tuned on the same folds reaches 97.36% +/- 0.42%, marking the headroom above the compact model. We report per-class precision, recall, F1, and pooled confusion matrices, diagnose and fix a one-class collapse caused by an unnormalized classifier head, and give a manual error analysis and bias-variance diagnosis showing that the compact CNNs under-fit.
  </details>

- **[GSCV++: Lightweight 3D Gaussian Splatting Sequence Compression via Standard Video Codecs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05359)**  `arXiv:2611.05359`  
  _Qi Yang, Yingzhan Xu, Kai Zhang, Li Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This paper presents GSCV++, a lightweight pipeline for 3D {G}aussian {S}platting (GS) sequences {C}ompression with standard {V}ideo codecs. To meet the requirements of low-complexity and hardware compatibility from the industry, GSCV++ jointly improves preprocessing, 3D-to-2D mapping, and practical video compression configuration of GS compression. First, for preprocessing, energy-adaptive inter-frame spherical harmonic (SH) alternative current (AC) dimensionality reduction (EA-SHDR) is proposed to produces a compact and stable temporal SH AC feature space. It can reduce the data volume of following mapping and practical compression, as well as less complexity while eliminating redundant information. Second, for 3D-to-2D mapping, prior-based Inter-MiniPLAS (P-MiniPLAS) is designed to generate smooth 2D feature maps through prior-based weighting factor inference and small scale remapping. Compared with vanilla Parallel Linear Assignment Sorting (PLAS) and Morton scan, P-MiniPLAS ensures the generated 2D maps are more suitable for the video codec while maintaining a low level of computational complexity. Third, for practical compression, content-aware generalized compression (CA-GC) is developed to use a non-uniform bit-depth split for coordinate map generation, and a coding unit size selection is introduced for efficient compression complexity control, which fully exploits the performance of video codecs without involving normative changes. Experiments demonstrate that GSCV++ reports over 20\% in intra- and 60\% in inter-prediction rate-distortion gains compared with state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods. GSCV++ also achieves faster 2D map generation, encoding, and decoding than other SOTA methods using the same type of video codecs. Ablation studies verify the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed modules and provide insights for subsequent research.
  </details>

- **[Diffusion Plug-in Priors for Self-Supervised Ultrasound Video Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05206)**  `arXiv:2611.05206`  
  _Chu Chen, Mingduo He, Yining Li, Yinhao Wu, Wei Tang, Yiwen Luo, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Ultrasound videos are limited by hardware-constrained spatial resolution and signal-dependent speckle, while paired low- and high-resolution acquisitions for supervised restoration are rarely available. We aim to jointly super-resolve and despeckle ultrasound videos without paired training data or network retraining. We propose UltraDiff, which uses a frozen pre-trained diffusion model (DM) as a plug-in prior and restores each frame by optimizing the latent seed of the DM sampler under a range-constrained maximum a posteriori formulation. We derive a speckle-aware data-fidelity term from a Rayleigh scale-family model and characterize its relative-residual and variance-adaptive weighting properties. We further introduce a stabilized fidelity that is bounded below and strictly convex in log intensity for positive observations in its unclamped form. A total-variation regularizer complements the DM prior, and a temporal seed update warm-starts each frame from the optimized seed of its predecessor. On EchoNet-LVH videos with simulated speckle and additive noise, UltraDiff with a natural-image prior improves PSNR by 1.24\,dB over the strongest baseline, and ultrasound-related priors improve it further. On real clinical echocardiography videos, UltraDiff reduces the mean absolute error of zero-shot ejection fraction (EF) prediction from 7.31 to 6.37 EF points relative to DUP, the strongest baseline. Combining a speckle-aware fidelity with frozen diffusion priors enables ground-truth-free ultrasound video restoration that benefits downstream measurement. UltraDiff needs neither paired data nor retraining and is not tied to a specific diffusion checkpoint. Code associated with this work will be made publicly available upon acceptance.
  </details>

- **[High-fidelity training-free photovoltaic module mapping from multi-view UAV imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05131)**  `arXiv:2611.05131`  
  _WenHao Duan, Qi Chen, Penglei Zhang, Ruizhe Deng, Zhiling Guo, Haoran Zhang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High-fidelity photovoltaic (PV) module mapping requires complete instance recovery and accurate delineation. Vision foundation models offer zero-shot segmentation, but converting incomplete and inconsistent predictions across views into a complete, geometrically accurate map of individual modules remains challenging. We propose the Multi-View PV Module Mapper (MV-PVMapper), a training-free framework coupling vision foundation models with unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) photogrammetry. First, Segment Anything Model 3 (SAM 3) generates candidates from original images using text prompts. Their contours are then transferred into object space through per-module slope correction to limit distortion from surface-model noise, and verified against representative module dimensions to guide cross-view instance selection. Finally, accepted instances return to all covering images as box prompts for SAM~3, supplementing text prompts with spatial cues for iterative segmentation, verification and fusion. On five-direction surveys of three operational rooftop PV plants at approximately 1 cm nadir ground sampling distance, two feedback iterations recover 7,710 of 7,711 reference modules with no false positives at an intersection-over-union (IoU) matching threshold of 0.5. Macro-averaged area precision and recall reach 99.00 % and 98.52 %, respectively. Averaged across sites, the aggregated Jaccard index reaches 97.55 %, compared with 60.73 % and 41.24 % for orthomosaic segmentation and multi-view pixel-voting baselines, respectively; average precision at the stricter IoU threshold of 0.95 reaches 95.67 %. Ablation studies show that module-size constraints are critical for instance separation, while slope correction and feedback to all covering views improve boundary accuracy.
